To determine how many packages of t-shirts Mom will have, we start by noting that each package contains 3 t-shirts, and she has a total of 51 t-shirts. The goal is to find how many packages of 3 t-shirts make up 51 t-shirts.

---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Understand the problem**:  
   We are given:
   - Total t-shirts: 51  
   - T-shirts per package: 3  
   - We need to find the number of packages.

2. **Set up the division**:  
   Since each package contains 3 t-shirts, the number of packages is calculated by dividing the total number of t-shirts by the number of t-shirts per package:

   $$
   \text{Number of packages} = \frac{51}{3}
   $$

3. **Perform the division**:  
   $$
   51 \div 3 = 17
   $$

4. **Verify the result**:  
   To confirm, multiply the number of packages by the number of t-shirts per package:
   $$
   17 \times 3 = 51
   $$
   This matches the total number of t-shirts, so the calculation is correct.

---

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{17}
$$
Answer: 17.0

Prediction:<think>
Okay, let's see. The problem says that Mom buys 70 white t-shirts in total. These t-shirts come in packages, and she buys 14 packages. The question is asking how many t-shirts are in each package. 

Hmm, so I need to find out the number of t-shirts per package. Let me think. If there are 14 packages and the total number of t-shirts is 70, then each package must have the same number of t-shirts, right? So this seems like a division problem. 

Total number of t-shirts divided by the number of packages should give the number per package. Let me write that down. So, 70 divided by 14. Let me do that calculation. 

Wait, 14 times 5 is 70. So 14 multiplied by 5 equals 70. That means if I divide 70 by 14, the result is 5. So each package has 5 t-shirts. 

Let me check if that makes sense. If each package has 5 t-shirts and there are 14 packages, then 14 times 5 is indeed 70. Yep, that adds up. 

I don't think there's any trick here. The problem seems straightforward. Mom buys 14 packages, total t-shirts are 70, so dividing 70 by 14 gives the number per package. 

Another way to think about it: if each package had 1 t-shirt, there would be 14 t-shirts. If each had 2, then 28. Each package with 3 would be 42. 4 packages would be 56. 5 packages would be 70. Oh, right, so 5 per package. 

So yeah, the answer should be 5. I don't see any other possible interpretation. The problem is pretty direct. No mention of different package sizes or anything. Just total t-shirts and number of packages. So division is the way to go. 

Final answer: 5 t-shirts per package.
</think>

To determine how many white t-shirts are in each package, we start with the total number of t-shirts and the number of packages:

- **Total t-shirts**: 70  
- **Number of packages**: 14  

We are told that the t-shirts are distributed equally across the packages. This means we need to divide the total number of t-shirts by the number of packages to find how many t-shirts are in each package:

$$
\text{Number of t-shirts per package} = \frac{\text{Total t-shirts}}{\text{Number of packages}} = \frac{70}{14}
$$

Performing the division:

$$
\frac{70}{14} = 5
$$

Thus, each package contains **5** white t-shirts.

---

**Final Answer:**  
$$
\boxed{5}
$$
Answer: 5.0

To solve the problem, we start by understanding the key information:

- There are **4 bags**.
- The total number of **cookies** is **36**.
- Each bag contains an **equal number** of cookies.

---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the total and the number of groups**:
   - Total cookies = 36
   - Number of bags = 4

2. **Determine the operation needed**:
   - Since the cookies are divided equally among the bags, we divide the total number of cookies by the number of bags.

3. **Perform the division**:
   $$
   \text{Cookies per bag} = \frac{36}{4} = 9
   $$

4. **Verify the result**:
   - If each bag has 9 cookies, then the total is:
     $$
     9 \times 4 = 36
     $$
   - This matches the given total, confirming the solution is correct.

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{9}

To determine the total number of cookies, we are given:

- Each bag contains **41 cookies**.
- There are **53 bags**.

We are asked to find the **total number of cookies**.

---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

This is a **multiplication problem** where we multiply the number of cookies per bag by the number of bags:

$$
\text{Total cookies} = \text{Cookies per bag} \times \text{Number of bags}
$$

Substituting the given values:

$$
\text{Total cookies} = 41 \times 53
$$

We can compute this using the distributive property:

$$
41 \times 53 = 41 \times (50 + 3) = (41 \times 50) + (41 \times 3)
$$

Now compute each part:

- $ 41 \times 50 = 2050 $
- $ 41 \times 3 = 123 $

Add the results:

$$
2050 + 123 = 2173
$$

To solve the problem, we start by identifying the total number of cookies and the number of bags:

- **Total cookies**: 14  
- **Number of bags**: 7  

Since the cookies are divided **equally** among the bags, we use **division** to determine how many cookies each bag contains. The formula is:

$$
\text{Cookies per bag} = \frac{\text{Total cookies}}{\text{Number of bags}} = \frac{14}{7}
$$

Performing the division:

$$
\frac{14}{7} = 2
$$

**Verification**:  
If each bag has 2 cookies and there are 7 bags, the total number of cookies is:

$$
2 \times 7 = 14
$$

This matches the given total, confirming the solution is correct.

**Final Answer**:  
Each bag has $\boxed{2}$ cookies.

To determine the total number of pieces of junk mail the mailman should deliver in each block, we start by analyzing the given information:

- Each house receives **9 pieces** of junk mail.
- There are **20 houses** on a single block.

The task is to find the total number of pieces of junk mail delivered **per block**. This requires multiplying the number of houses on a block by the number of pieces each house receives:

$$
\text{Total pieces per block} = \text{Number of houses} \times \text{Pieces per house}
$$

$$
\text{Total pieces per block} = 20 \times 9 = 180
$$

$$
\boxed{180}
$$

To determine how many pieces of junk mail the mailman should give to each house, we start by analyzing the given information:

- There are **6 houses** on a block.
- The mailman has to deliver a total of **24 pieces of junk mail** to the **entire block**.

Since the junk mail is to be distributed **equally among the 6 houses**, we divide the total number of pieces by the number of houses:

$$
\text{Junk mail per house} = \frac{\text{Total junk mail}}{\text{Number of houses}} = \frac{24}{6} = 4
$$

If each house receives 4 pieces, then the total for all 6 houses would be:

$$
6 \times 4 = 24
$$

This matches the total amount of junk mail specified for the block, confirming the calculation is correct.

$$
\boxed{4}
$$

To determine the total number of pieces of junk mail the mailman should deliver to each block, we need to calculate the total based on the number of houses and the amount of mail per house.

---

### Step-by-Step Breakdown:

- **Number of houses in a block:** 20  
- **Pieces of junk mail per house:** 32  

To find the total number of pieces of junk mail for the entire block, we multiply the number of houses by the number of pieces per house:

$$
\text{Total junk mail} = 20 \times 32
$$

We can compute this as:

$$
20 \times 32 = (20 \times 30) + (20 \times 2) = 600 + 40 = 640
$$

---

### Final Answer:

$$
\boxed{640}
$$

To determine how many rounds Luke played, we start by analyzing the given information:

- **Total points scored by Luke:** 154  
- **Points gained per round:** 11  

We are told that Luke gained **11 points in each round**, and we are to find the **number of rounds** he played. This is a classic **division problem**, where the total is divided by the value per unit to find the number of units.

---

### Step-by-step Calculation:

$$
\text{Number of rounds} = \frac{\text{Total points}}{\text{Points per round}} = \frac{154}{11}

To determine the total number of beads Emily used, we need to calculate the product of the number of necklaces and the number of beads per necklace. 

Emily made **11 necklaces**, and each necklace requires **28 beads**. This is a straightforward multiplication problem:

$$
\text{Total beads} = \text{Number of necklaces} \times \text{Beads per necklace}
$$

$$
\text{Total beads} = 11 \times 28
$$

Breaking down the multiplication:

$$
11 \times 28 = (10 \times 28) + (1 \times 28) = 280 + 28 = 308
$$

Thus, the total number of beads Emily used is:

$$
\boxed{308}

Emily is making bead necklaces for her 44 friends. She made **26 necklaces**, and each necklace requires **2 beads**. The question asks how many beads Emily had, which refers to the total number of beads used for the necklaces.

---

### Step-by-Step Breakdown:

1. **Number of necklaces made**: 26  
2. **Beads per necklace**: 2  
3. **Total beads used** = Number of necklaces × Beads per necklace  
   $$
   26 \times 2 = 52
   $$

The mention of Emily having **44 friends** is context but does not affect the calculation since the problem specifies that she made **26 necklaces**, not one per friend. Therefore, the total number of beads used (and thus the number of beads Emily had) is based on the 26 necklaces.

---

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{52}
$$
Answer: 52.0

$$
\text{Kids staying home} = \text{Total kids} - \text{Kids at camp}

$$
\text{Kids staying home} = 898,051 - 629,424
$$

---

- **Adam and Jackie together have 7 apples.**  
  This means the total number of apples that Adam and Jackie have combined is 7.

- **Someone (let's call them Person X) has 9 apples more than Adam and Jackie together.**  
  This means Person X has **9 more apples than the total number of apples Adam and Jackie have together**.

---

### Step 2: Set Up the Equation

- $ A $: number of apples Adam has  
- $ J $: number of apples Jackie has  
- $ X $: number of apples Person X has

$$
A + J = 7
$$

$$

$$

$$
X = 7 + 9 = 16
$$

\boxed{16}

Only **Fred** washed cars, so we calculate the difference in his money:

$$
\text{Earnings from washing cars} = \text{Final amount} - \text{Initial amount} = 115 - 111 = 4
$$

---

### Step 4: Interpret the Question

The question asks: **"How much money did they earn by washing cars?"**

- "They" refers to **both Fred and Jason**, but only **Fred** washed cars.
- Therefore, the total amount earned by **washing cars** is **only Fred's earnings**.

---

### Final Answer

$$
\boxed{4}
$$
Answer: 4.0

- **Current weight**: 27 kilograms  
- **Weight lost**: 101 kilograms

Jessie initially weighed 114 kilograms. Over two weeks, she lost weight as follows:

1. **First week**: Lost 50 kg.  
   $ 114 - 50 = 64 $ kg remaining.  
2. **Second week**: Lost 60 kg.  
   $ 64 - 60 = 4 $ kg remaining.  

**Total weight loss**: $ 50 + 60 = 110 $ kg.  
**Final weight**: $ 114 - 110 = 4 $ kg.  

**Answer:** Jessie now weighs $\boxed{4}$ kilograms.

To solve the problem, we begin by identifying the total number of animals in the cage and the number of animals that are hiding.

---

### Step 1: Total Number of Animals
The problem states that the total number of animals in the cage is:

$$
79
$$

These animals consist of **snakes** and **alligators**.

---

### Step 2: Number of Hiding Animals
We are told that:

- **24 snakes** are hiding
- **51 alligators** are hiding

Since snakes and alligators are distinct animals, we can safely add these two quantities to find the total number of animals that are hiding:

$$
24 + 51 = 75
$$

---

### Step 3: Number of Animals Not Hiding
To find the number of animals that are **not hiding**, we subtract the number of hiding animals from the total number of animals:

$$
79 - 75 = 4
$$

---

### Final Answer

$$
\boxed{4}
$$
Answer: 4.0

Let me calculate that. 171 minus 134... Let me do the subtraction. 171 minus 100 is 71, then minus 34 more. Wait, maybe it's easier to do it straight. 171 - 134. Let me subtract 130 first, which would be 171 - 130 = 41, then subtract 4 more, so 41 - 4 = 37. So after yesterday, he has 37 tomatoes left.

Then today, he picked 30 more. So we need to subtract 30 from the remaining 37. Let me do that. 37 minus 30 is 7. So after picking 30 today, he would have 7 tomatoes left.

Let me check. 134 + 30 is 164, then +7 is 171. Yes, that's correct. So after yesterday, 37 left. Then today he picks 30, so 37 - 30 = 7. That seems right.

Then subtract that from the original 171. So 171 - 164. Let me compute that. 171 - 160 is 11, then subtract 4 more, so 11 - 4 = 7. Same answer. So that confirms it. So the answer is 7.

</think>

The farmer initially had 171 tomatoes. He picked 134 tomatoes yesterday and 30 today. To find the remaining tomatoes:

1. **Total picked**:  
   $ 134 \, (\text{yesterday}) + 30 \, (\text{today}) = 164 $.

2. **Remaining tomatoes**:  
   $ 171 \, (\text{original}) - 164 \, (\text{picked}) = 7 $.

**Final Answer:**  
The farmer will have $\boxed{7}$ tomatoes left after today.
Answer: 7.0

To determine how many planks John needs for the house wall, we start by analyzing the relationship between the number of nails and the number of planks.

---

### Step 1: Understand the Given Information

- **Total nails used**: 4
- **Nails required per plank**: 2

This means that for every plank, John uses exactly 2 nails.

---

### Step 2: Set Up the Equation

Let the number of planks be $ P $. Since each plank requires 2 nails, the total number of nails used is:

$$
\text{Total nails} = 2 \times P
$$

We are told the total number of nails is 4, so:

$$
2 \times P = 4
$$

---

### Step 3: Solve for $ P $

$$
P = \frac{4}{2} = 2
$$

---

$$
\boxed{2}
$$

- **John is using 16 planks.**

Question:robin has some packages of gum . there are 3 pieces in each package and a total of 42 pieces of gum . how many packages does robin have ?
Prediction:<think>
Okay, let's see. Robin has some packages of gum. Each package has 3 pieces, and the total number of pieces is 42. I need to find out how many packages Robin has. 

Hmm, so the problem is about dividing the total number of gum pieces by the number of pieces per package. That makes sense because each package contributes 3 pieces. So if I divide the total by the number per package, I should get the number of packages. 

Let me write that down. Total pieces are 42. Each package has 3. So the number of packages is 42 divided by 3. Let me do that calculation. 42 divided by 3. Well, 3 times 14 is 42, right? Because 3 times 10 is 30, 3 times 4 is 12, so 30 + 12 is 42. So 14 packages. 

Wait, is there another way to check? Let me multiply 14 packages by 3 pieces each. 14 times 3. 10 times 3 is 30, 4 times 3 is 12, so 30 + 12 is 42. Yep, that matches the total. So that seems correct. 

I don't think there's any trick here. The problem seems straightforward. Maybe I need to make sure that there are no leftover pieces or something? But the problem says a total of 42 pieces, and each package is exactly 3. So 42 divided by 3 is exactly 14. So there's no remainder. 

So the answer should be 14 packages. I think that's it. Let me just recap. Total pieces divided by pieces per package equals number of packages. 42 divided by 3 is 14. Yep, that's the answer.
</think>

To determine how many packages of gum Robin has, we start by analyzing the given information:

- Each package contains **3 pieces** of gum.
- The **total number of pieces** is **42**.

We are asked to find the **number of packages**. This is a division problem, where we divide the total number of pieces by the number of pieces per package:

$$
\text{Number of packages} = \frac{\text{Total pieces}}{\text{Pieces per package}} = \frac{42}{3}
$$

Performing the division:

$$
\frac{42}{3} = 14
$$

To verify, we can multiply the number of packages by the number of pieces per package:

$$
14 \times 3 = 42
$$

This confirms the calculation is correct.

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{14}
$$

Wait, that seems straightforward. Let me write that equation out:

Total gum = (number of packages × pieces per package) + extra pieces

So, 7x + 6 = 41.

To solve for x, subtract 6 from both sides:

7x = 41 - 6

Which is 7x = 35.

Then divide both sides by 7:

x = 35 / 7 = 5.

So, Robin has 5 packages. Let me check if that makes sense.

If there are 5 packages, each with 7 pieces, that's 5×7=35. Then adding the 6 extra pieces gives 35+6=41, which matches the total given. So that seems right.

Let me think again. Maybe there's a trick? Like, maybe the extra pieces are part of the packages? But no, the problem says "6 extra pieces of gum," so they are separate from the packages. To determine how many packages of gum Robin has, we start by analyzing the given information:

- Each package contains **7 pieces** of gum.
- Robin has **6 extra pieces** of gum.
- The **total number of gum pieces** Robin has is **41**.

---

### Step 1: Define the unknown
Let the number of packages Robin has be $ x $.

---

### Step 2: Set up the equation
The total number of gum pieces is the sum of:
- The gum from the packages: $ 7x $
- The extra pieces: $ 6 $

So, the equation becomes:
$$
7x + 6 = 41
$$

---

### Step 3: Solve the equation
Subtract 6 from both sides:
$$
7x = 41 - 6 = 35
$$

Now divide both sides by 7:
$$
x = \frac{35}{7} = 5
$$

---

### Step 4: Verify the solution
- 5 packages × 7 pieces per package = 35 pieces
- Add the 6 extra pieces: $ 35 + 6 = 41 $, which matches the total.

---

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{5}
$$
Answer: 5.0

To determine how many minutes the machine was working, we start by analyzing the given information:

- The machine produces **3 shirts per minute**.
- It made a total of **6 shirts**.

We are asked to find the **time** (in minutes) the machine was working. 

---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Understand the rate**:  
   The machine produces **3 shirts per minute**. This means that for every minute of operation, it makes 3 shirts.

2. **Determine the total quantity**:  
   The machine made **6 shirts** in total.

3. **Use the formula for time**:  
   Since the rate is given as shirts per minute, we can use the formula:  
   $$
   \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Total Shirts}}{\text{Rate}}
   $$

4. **Plug in the values**:  
   $$
   \text{Time} = \frac{6 \text{ shirts}}{3 \text{ shirts per minute}} = 2 \text{ minutes}
   $$

To determine how many minutes the machine worked in total, we begin by analyzing the rate at which the machine produces shirts and the total number of shirts made over the two days.

---

### Step 1: Understand the Rate
The machine produces **3 shirts per minute**. This is the key rate we'll use to calculate the time spent working.

---

### Step 2: Calculate Time for Each Day

- **Yesterday**: The machine made **4 shirts**.  
  Time = $ \frac{4 \text{ shirts}}{3 \text{ shirts/minute}} = \frac{4}{3} $ minutes.

- **Today**: The machine made **8 shirts**.  
  Time = $ \frac{8 \text{ shirts}}{3 \text{ shirts/minute}} = \frac{8}{3} $ minutes.

---

### Step 3: Add the Times

Total time = $ \frac{4}{3} + \frac{8}{3} = \frac{12}{3} = 4 $ minutes.

---

### Step 4: Alternative Verification

We can also verify by summing the total number of shirts made:

- Total shirts = $ 4 + 8 = 12 $
- Total time = $ \frac{12 \text{ shirts}}{3 \text{ shirts/minute}} = 4 $ minutes

This confirms the result.

---

### Final Answer

The machine worked for a total of $\boxed{4}$ minutes.
Answer: 4.0

This is a straightforward division problem:  
$$
\text{Daily earnings} = \frac{\text{Total earnings}}{\text{Number of days}} = \frac{165}{5}
$$

Performing the division:  
$$

$$

$$
33 \times 5 = 165
$$

$$

To determine the total amount of money made by the ring toss game, we need to calculate the product of the daily earnings and the number of days the game was operated.

---

### Step-by-Step Breakdown:

- **Daily earnings**: $144
- **Number of days**: 22

We use the formula:

$$
\text{Total Earnings} = \text{Daily Earnings} \times \text{Number of Days}
$$

To determine the number of pupils present in the program, we start by understanding the relationship between the total number of people, the number of parents, and the number of pupils. The total number of people includes both parents and pupils. Therefore, we can express this as:

$$
\text{Total people} = \text{Parents} + \text{Pupils}
$$

We are given:
- Total people = 676
- Parents = 22

To find the number of pupils, we rearrange the equation:

$$
\text{Pupils} = \text{Total people} - \text{Parents}
$$

Substituting the given values:

$$
\text{Pupils} = 676 - 22
$$

Performing the subtraction:

$$
676 - 22 = 654
$$

Thus, the number of pupils present in the program is:

$$
\boxed{654}
$$
Answer: 654.0

To determine how much farther they need to go to reach the end of the cave, we start by understanding the given information:

- The **total depth** of the cave is **762 feet**.
- They are currently at **659 feet**.

The question asks for the **remaining distance** from their current position to the bottom of the cave. This can be calculated by subtracting the distance they have already descended from the total depth of the cave:

$$
\text{Remaining Distance} = \text{Total Depth} - \text{Current Depth}
$$

Substituting the given values:

$$
\text{Remaining Distance} = 762 - 659
$$

Performing the subtraction:

$$
762 - 659 = 103
$$

---

### Final Answer:
$$
\boxed{103}
$$

They need to descend **103 feet** more to reach the end of the cave.

To determine the total number of items Adam's shelves can hold, we begin by analyzing the information provided:

- Each shelf can hold **8 action figures** and **10 cabinets**.
- There are **4 shelves** in total.

---

### Step 1: Calculate the number of items per shelf

Each shelf contains both action figures and cabinets. So, the total number of items on a single shelf is:

$$
8 \text{ (action figures)} + 10 \text{ (cabinets)} = 18 \text{ items per shelf}
$$

---

### Step 2: Multiply by the number of shelves

Since there are 4 shelves, and each shelf holds 18 items, the total number of items that can be held across all shelves is:

$$
18 \times 4 = 72
$$

---

### Step 3: Verification (Alternative Method)

We can also verify this by calculating the total number of action figures and cabinets separately:

- Total action figures:  
  $$
  8 \text{ per shelf} \times 4 \text{ shelves} = 32
  $$

- Total cabinets:  
  $$
  10 \text{ per shelf} \times 4 \text{ shelves} = 40
  $$

Adding these together:

$$
32 + 40 = 72
$$

---

### Final Answer

$$
\boxed{72}
$$
Answer: 72.0

To solve the problem, we are given:

- There are **141 pots**.
- Each pot contains **71 flowers** and **91 sticks**.
- The question asks for the **total number of flowers**.

---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

We are only concerned with the **number of flowers**, not the sticks. Therefore, we can ignore the sticks and focus on the flowers.

Each pot has **71 flowers**, and there are **141 pots**. To find the total number of flowers, we multiply:

$$
\text{Total flowers} = \text{Number of pots} \times \text{Flowers per pot} = 141 \times 71
$$

---

### Performing the Multiplication:

We can compute $ 141 \times 71 $ using the distributive property:

$$
141 \times 71 = 141 \times (70 + 1) = (141 \times 70) + (141 \times 1)
$$

- $ 141 \times 70 = 9870 $
- $ 141 \times 1 = 141 $

Now, add the two results:

$$
9870 + 141 = 10011
$$

---

### Final Answer:

$$
\boxed{10011}
$$
Answer: 10011.0

To determine the total number of students in the school, we are given the following information:

- There are **304 grades**.
- Each grade has **75 students**.

---

### Step 1: Understand the Problem

We are asked to find the **total number of students**, which means we need to calculate the **sum of students across all grades**. Since each grade has the same number of students, this is a **multiplication problem**:

$$
\text{Total students} = \text{Number of grades} \times \text{Students per grade}
$$

$$
\text{Total students} = 304 \times 75
$$

---

### Step 2: Perform the Multiplication

We can break this multiplication into smaller parts for clarity:

$$
304 \times 75 = 304 \times (70 + 5) = (304 \times 70) + (304 \times 5)
$$

- $ 304 \times 70 = 21280 $
- $ 304 \times 5 = 1520 $

Now, add the two results:

$$
21280 + 1520 = 22800
$$

---

### Step 3: Final Answer

Thus, the total number of students in the school is:

$$
\boxed{22800}
$$
Answer: 22800.0
